Pareto plot artifact registered.\\\"}, \\\"exp_adaptive_sampling_budget\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"PREPARING\\\", \\\"retry_count\\\": 0, \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T09:29:22.761Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T09:29:41.158Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Confidence-aware adaptive sampling budget allocation (fewer samples for high-confidence instances, full N=8 for low-confidence) can recover nearly all SC F1 gains while saving 40-50% of inference cost, because high-confidence instances already have low diversity and gain nothing from additional samples.\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"model\\\": \\\"Qwen3-8B FT\\\", \\\"fewnerd\\\": {\\\"n_instances\\\": 37648, \\\"greedy_f1\\\": 0.7884, \\\"uniform_n8_f1\\\": 0.7819, \\\"best_adaptive\\\": \\\"h1_l8\\\", \\\"best_adaptive_f1\\\": 0.7849, \\\"best_delta_vs_u8\\\": \\\"+0.30pp\\\", \\\"sample_savings\\\": \\\"43.8%\\\", \\\"note\\\": \\\"Greedy > U8; adaptive h1/l8 best: greedy for high-conf, N=8 SC for low-conf\\\"}, \\\"scierc\\\": {\\\"n_instances\\\": 551, \\\"greedy_f1\\\": 0.6592, \\\"uniform_n8_f1\\\": 0.6712, \\\"best_adaptive\\\": \\\"top67%_h2_l8\\\", \\\"best_adaptive_f1\\\": 0.6707, \\\"best_delta_vs_u8\\\": \\\"-0.05pp\\\", \\\"sample_savings\\\": \\\"50.2%\\\", \\\"note\\\": \\\"U8 > Greedy; top67% h2/l8 saves 50% with negligible loss\\\"}}, \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"success\\\", \\\"notes\\\": \\\"B7 adaptive sampling budget analysis on Qwen3-8B FT. Key findings: (1) FewNERD: greedy already beats uniform N=8 SC (+0.65pp), adaptive h1/l8 is optimal (+0.30pp vs U8, saves 43.8% samples); (2) SciERC: U8 beats greedy (+1.20pp), top67% h2/l8 saves 50% with only -0.05pp loss. Practical implication: confidence-gated sampling can halve inference cost. Artifacts: vc_8b_qwen3_ft_results, sj_8b_qwen3_ft_results (registered by worker).\\\"}, \\\"exp_dgec_sj_construction\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"PREPARING\\\", \\\"retry_count\\\": 0, \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T08:37:58.013Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T09:26:30.567Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"SJ-weighted entity construction serves as control for VC — if SJ (soft entity overlap) works as well as VC (exact match consensus), any stronger QE signal suffices for construction; if VC specifically outperforms SJ, exact-match consensus has a unique advantage over soft similarity for entity-level selection.\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"total_configs\\\": 26, \\\"significant_positive\\\": 1, \\\"significant_negative\\\": 15, \\\"not_significant\\\": 10, \\\"best_config\\\": \\\"scierc/72b/zs +2.0pp p=0.001\\\", \\\"worst_config\\\": \\\"fewnerd/8b_qwen3/ft mean -1.48pp all seeds p=1.0\\\", \\\"qwen3_8b_ft_supplement\\\": {\\\"configs\\\": 9, \\\"sig+\\\": 0, \\\"sig-\\\": 6, \\\"ns\\\": 3, \\\"fewnerd_mean_delta\\\": -1.48, \\\"scierc_mean_delta\\\": -0.4, \\\"conll_mean_delta\\\": -0.5, \\\"note\\\": \\\"SJ fully negative on Qwen3-8B FT — consistent with Qwen2.5-7B FT pattern\\\"}, \\\"m030_all_passed\\\": true}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"COMPLETE (26 configs). SJ-weighted construction: 1 sig+ / 15 sig- / 10 ns. Qwen3-8B FT supplement (9 configs): 0+/6-/3ns — fully negative, consistent with 7B FT pattern. FewNERD -1.48pp mean (all seeds p=1.0). Definitively answers control question: NOT any stronger QE signal works for construction. SJ introduces noise at entity level. Only exception: SciERC 72B ZS (lowest precision, lowest degeneracy). Closed per D170/D171.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp_dgec_vc_construction\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"PREPARING\\\", \\\"retry_count\\\": 0, \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T08:37:55.968Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T09:26:29.776Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"VC-weighted entity construction outperforms greedy and LP-weighted construction across configs because Vote Count is a more direct quality estimator for structured outputs — it measures exact-match consensus without relying on model-internal log probabilities. Especially effective in high-degeneracy regimes where LP compression makes LP-weighting uninformative.\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"total_configs\\\": 28, \\\"significant_positive\\\": 9, \\\"significant_negative\\\": 10, \\\"not_significant\\\": 9, \\\"by_dataset\\\": {\\\"fewnerd\\\": {\\\"configs\\\": 11, \\\"sig+\\\": 5, \\\"sig-\\\": 5, \\\"ns\\\": 1, \\\"mean_delta_pp\\\": -0.12}, \\\"wnut17\\\": {\\\"configs\\\": 3, \\\"sig+\\\": 2, \\\"sig-\\\": 0, \\\"ns\\\": 1, \\\"mean_delta_pp\\\": 0.92}, \\\"scierc\\\": {\\\"configs\\\": 7, \\\"sig+\\\": 2, \\\"sig-\\\": 0, \\\"ns\\\": 5, \\\"mean_delta_pp\\\": 0.62}, \\\"conll\\\": {\\\"configs\\\": 7, \\\"sig+\\\": 0, \\\"sig-\\\": 5, \\\"ns\\\": 2, \\\"mean_delta_pp\\\": -0.33}}, \\\"qwen3_8b_ft_supplement\\\": {\\\"configs\\\": 9, \\\"sig+\\\": 3, \\\"sig-\\\": 3, \\\"ns\\\": 3, \\\"best\\\": \\\"SciERC s42 +1.40pp p=0.017\\\", \\\"worst\\\": \\\"CoNLL s456 -0.54pp p=0.998\\\", \\\"note\\\": \\\"VC inconsistent on LP-success configs; seed-dependent on FewNERD; CoNLL all negative due to high degeneracy\\\"}, \\\"m030_all_passed\\\": true}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"COMPLETE (28 configs). VC-weighted construction: 9 sig+ / 10 sig- / 9 ns. Qwen3-8B FT supplement (9 configs): 3+/3-/3ns — VC inconsistent even on LP-success configs. SciERC s42 best (+1.40pp) but cross-seed unstable. CoNLL all negative (high degeneracy). WNUT17/SciERC lean positive but not robust. Key answer: VC does NOT reliably improve where LP already works — seed-dependent, dataset-dependent. DGEC unified method goal (70-80% configs) unreachable at 32% (9/28). Closed per D170/D171.\\\", \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\"}, \\\"exp_dgec_heterogeneous_t\\\": {\\\"status\\\": \\\"PREPARING\\\", \\\"retry_count\\\": 0, \\\"created_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T08:37:59.963Z\\\", \\\"updated_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-20T08:49:58.009Z\\\", \\\"hypothesis\\\": \\\"Mixing samples from T=0.7 and T=1.0 significantly reduces degeneracy compared to single-T sampling (by breaking temperature-induced mode collapse), enabling entity construction to achieve significant ΔF1 improvement even in configs where single-T construction was n.s. due to high degeneracy.\\\", \\\"metrics\\\": {\\\"dataset\\\": \\\"SciERC\\\", \\\"n_instances\\\": 529, \\\"t07_degen\\\": 0.2231, \\\"t10_degen\\\": 0.1235, \\\"mixed_degen\\\": 0.1701, \\\"mixed_uniform_f1\\\": 0.6584, \\\"mixed_vc_f1\\\": 0.6609, \\\"cross_pool_uniform_delta\\\": \\\"+0.006 (1/3 seeds sig)\\\", \\\"cross_pool_vc_delta\\\": \\\"+0.004 (1/3 seeds sig)\\\", \\\"t08_t10_result\\\": \\\"harmful (degen increased)\\\", \\\"conclusion_preliminary\\\": \\\"partially_supported\\\"}, \\\"notes\\\": \\\"Hypothesis partially supported. T=0.7+T=1.0 mixed pool reduces degen (22.3%→17.0%) and improves construction (VC 3/3 seeds sig vs greedy), but cross-pool comparison (mixed vs T=1.0 alone) only 1/3 seeds significant. T gap matters: ΔT=0.2 (T=0.8+T=1.0) harmful, ΔT=0.3 (T=0.7+T=1.0) beneficial. VC weighting confirmed cross-T compatible. Awaiting Director conclusion classification.\\\"}}, \\\"count\\\": 20, \\\"total\\\": 20, \\\"baseline\\\": null}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.6565}}\"}",

      "result": "{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"experiments\": {\"exp_e2_pretrained_conll\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-21T05:52:08.180Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-21T05:53:27.266Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"E2 pre-trained baseline: Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct (unfinetuned) on CoNLL SC pipeline N=8 T=1.0. Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct base model, 200 dev examples, N=8 T=1.0, execution accuracy evaluation. If F1-F4 failure modes exist in SQL, the framework generalizes beyond NER.\", \"server\": \"autodl-5090-b\", \"gpu\": \"cuda:2\"}, \"exp_b2_v4_inverse_frequency\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T12:50:51.737Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T12:51:06.312Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Inverse-frequency weighting of entity features (agreement_count, vc, sj normalized by log(1+type_freq)) improves cross-domain generalization by reducing dominance of high-frequency entity types. Two variants: A) replace original features, B) augment as additional features.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\", \"metrics\": {\"variant_a_replace_fold3_lgb_auc\": 0.834, \"variant_a_replace_fold3_ece\": 0.2188, \"variant_a_f1_delta\": -0.013, \"variant_b_augment_fold3_lgb_auc\": 0.8541, \"variant_b_augment_fold3_ece\": 0.1934, \"variant_b_f1_delta\": -0.009, \"sj_ifw_std_reduction\": \"54% (0.138→0.063)\", \"ac_ifw_std_reduction\": \"10% (0.122→0.110)\", \"verdict\": \"IFW improves stability but reduces discriminative power. Augment variant neutral. Replace variant harmful.\"}, \"parent\": \"exp_b2_entity_verifier\"}, \"exp_b2_v3_domain_invariant\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T12:50:49.948Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T12:51:00.972Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Removing domain-specific features (dataset_enc, entity_type distributions) forces the verifier to rely on domain-invariant signals, improving cross-domain generalization at the cost of some discriminative power. If LGB AUC improves on held-out domains, domain features were causing negative transfer.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\", \"metrics\": {\"fold3_fewnerd_lgb_auc\": 0.8562, \"fold3_fewnerd_xgb_auc\": 0.8518, \"fold3_ece\": 0.1373, \"fold2_scierc_lgb_auc\": 0.8152, \"fold2_scierc_xgb_auc\": 0.8163, \"vs_v2_fold3_lgb_delta\": 0.0085, \"vs_v2_fold2_lgb_delta\": 0.0146, \"top_features\": \"agreement_count (58.4%) + vc (30.3%) = 88%\", \"verdict\": \"LGB AUC improved by removing domain features, confirming negative transfer. But ECE still high, F1 delta still negative.\"}, \"parent\": \"exp_b2_entity_verifier\"}, \"exp_b2_v2_multi_domain_joint\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T12:50:09.686Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T12:50:18.610Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Joint multi-domain training (CoNLL+SciERC+FewNERD LODO) improves entity verifier AUC and cross-domain construction F1 by exposing the model to diverse entity patterns. Tests whether training data diversity overcomes the calibration gap that caused B2 original to fail on FewNERD.\", \"conclusion\": \"negative\", \"metrics\": {\"fold3_fewnerd_lgb_auc\": 0.8477, \"fold3_fewnerd_xgb_auc\": 0.8603, \"fold3_fewnerd_f1_delta\": -0.008, \"fold3_ece\": 0.136, \"fold4_iid_ece\": 0.007, \"vs_b2_original_lgb_auc_delta\": 0.014, \"vs_b2_original_xgb_auc_delta\": 0.032, \"verdict\": \"AUC improved but F1 still negative. Cross-domain calibration gap unsolved.\"}, \"parent\": \"exp_b2_entity_verifier\"}, \"exp_b2_entity_verifier\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T10:13:41.037Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T12:28:21.280Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"An XGBoost/LightGBM entity-level verifier trained on agreement_count, VC, LP features can predict whether entity candidates are in gold annotations, enabling post-hoc filtering or score-weighted construction that improves over greedy baseline, with cross-dataset generalization from CoNLL+SciERC to FewNERD+WNUT.\", \"metrics\": {\"oof_auc\": 0.9233, \"oof_path_a_f1_delta\": 0.003, \"oof_path_b_f1_delta\": -0.0157, \"oof_greedy_f1\": 0.8216, \"oof_path_a_f1\": 0.8246, \"oof_majority_vote_f1\": 0.8048, \"leakage_pct\": 91, \"original_delta_pp\": 3.62, \"oof_delta_pp\": 0.3, \"per_dataset\": {\"scierc\": \"+1.28pp\", \"conll\": \"+0.55pp\", \"fewnerd\": \"+0.02pp\"}, \"verdict\": \"B2 verifier gain was 91% leakage. OOF Path A +0.30pp not significant. NOT suitable for paper §5.\", \"n_inverse_scaling_validation\": {\"subset_n\": 4351, \"full_n\": 32565, \"subset_greedy_f1\": 0.7926, \"full_greedy_f1\": 0.7913, \"verdict\": \"Not a bug. Representative subset. Paper should note subset size.\"}}, \"conclusion\": \"negative\"}, \"exp_a2_degen_regression\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T10:13:37.615Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T10:13:55.891Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Degeneracy rate is primarily driven by task difficulty (dataset entropy ceiling) rather than model capability, with a positive correlation between base F1 and degeneracy — easy tasks with high F1 produce degenerate outputs because the model has already converged.\", \"metrics\": {\"n_points\": 30, \"linear_r2\": 0.414, \"quadratic_r2\": 0.592, \"spearman_rho\": 0.519, \"spearman_p\": 0.0033, \"linear_coeff\": \"degen = 129.5 × F1 - 56.9\", \"key_finding\": \"degeneracy driven by dataset difficulty (entropy ceiling), not model capability\"}, \"conclusion\": \"success\"}, \"exp_e1_signal_routing\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T10:13:34.839Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T10:13:52.098Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"A config-level signal router (RF/LR) can predict the optimal construction signal per config from features (degeneracy_rate, base_f1, oracle_headroom), outperforming always-LP baseline. Router ΔF1 should exceed always-LP ΔF1.\", \"metrics\": {\"n_configs\": 16, \"rf_loo_accuracy\": 0.562, \"router_avg_delta_f1\": 0.39, \"always_lp_delta_f1\": 0.15, \"oracle_delta_f1\": 0.56, \"top_features\": [\"oracle_headroom\", \"degeneracy_rate\", \"base_f1\"], \"label_dist\": {\"lp_selection\": 6, \"none\": 5, \"consensus_theta2n\": 3, \"consensus_lp_weighted\": 1, \"majority_vote\": 1}}, \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"notes\": \"E1 signal routing. RF 56.2% LOO accuracy (below 60% target) but router ΔF1 +0.39pp > always-LP +0.15pp (2.6× improvement, target met). Low accuracy due to small n=16 and class imbalance. D176: appendix-only, not main claim.\"}, \"exp_pareto_diversity_quality\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T09:31:45.421Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T09:31:58.776Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"There exists a Pareto frontier in the diversity (1-degeneracy) vs construction quality (ΔF1) space across temperature configurations. Well-separated temperature mixing (T=0.7+T=1.0) achieves Pareto optimality for both uniform and VC construction, while VC-weighted construction flattens the trade-off slope, reducing the diversity cost of quality.\", \"metrics\": {\"dataset\": \"SciERC\", \"configs\": {\"t07\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.777, \"uniform_delta\": 1.81, \"vc_delta\": 1.17, \"pareto\": \"uniform only\"}, \"t08\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.819, \"uniform_delta\": 1.25, \"vc_delta\": 1.68, \"pareto\": \"VC only\"}, \"mix_07_10\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.83, \"uniform_delta\": 1.43, \"vc_delta\": 1.67, \"pareto\": \"both\"}, \"t10\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.877, \"uniform_delta\": 0.75, \"vc_delta\": 1.21, \"pareto\": \"both (highest diversity)\"}, \"mix_08_10\": {\"one_minus_degen\": 0.789, \"uniform_delta\": -0.17, \"vc_delta\": 0.47, \"pareto\": \"dominated\"}}, \"vc_slope_reduction\": \"~50% flatter than uniform\", \"key_insight\": \"well-separated T mixing dual Pareto optimal; close T mixing dominated\"}, \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"notes\": \"A4 Pareto diversity-quality analysis on SciERC. Mix T=0.7+T=1.0 is dual-Pareto optimal (uniform +1.43pp, VC +1.67pp). VC construction flattens trade-off slope ~50% vs uniform — increasing diversity costs less quality. Mix T=0.8+T=1.0 is dominated (close T → higher degeneracy, no construction gain). Confirms DGEC heterogeneous-T finding: ΔT matters, ΔT=0.3 beneficial, ΔT=0.2 harmful. Pareto plot artifact registered.\"}, \"exp_adaptive_sampling_budget\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T09:29:22.761Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T09:29:41.158Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"Confidence-aware adaptive sampling budget allocation (fewer samples for high-confidence instances, full N=8 for low-confidence) can recover nearly all SC F1 gains while saving 40-50% of inference cost, because high-confidence instances already have low diversity and gain nothing from additional samples.\", \"metrics\": {\"model\": \"Qwen3-8B FT\", \"fewnerd\": {\"n_instances\": 37648, \"greedy_f1\": 0.7884, \"uniform_n8_f1\": 0.7819, \"best_adaptive\": \"h1_l8\", \"best_adaptive_f1\": 0.7849, \"best_delta_vs_u8\": \"+0.30pp\", \"sample_savings\": \"43.8%\", \"note\": \"Greedy > U8; adaptive h1/l8 best: greedy for high-conf, N=8 SC for low-conf\"}, \"scierc\": {\"n_instances\": 551, \"greedy_f1\": 0.6592, \"uniform_n8_f1\": 0.6712, \"best_adaptive\": \"top67%_h2_l8\", \"best_adaptive_f1\": 0.6707, \"best_delta_vs_u8\": \"-0.05pp\", \"sample_savings\": \"50.2%\", \"note\": \"U8 > Greedy; top67% h2/l8 saves 50% with negligible loss\"}}, \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"notes\": \"B7 adaptive sampling budget analysis on Qwen3-8B FT. Key findings: (1) FewNERD: greedy already beats uniform N=8 SC (+0.65pp), adaptive h1/l8 is optimal (+0.30pp vs U8, saves 43.8% samples); (2) SciERC: U8 beats greedy (+1.20pp), top67% h2/l8 saves 50% with only -0.05pp loss. Practical implication: confidence-gated sampling can halve inference cost. Artifacts: vc_8b_qwen3_ft_results, sj_8b_qwen3_ft_results (registered by worker).\"}, \"exp_dgec_sj_construction\": {\"status\": \"PREPARING\", \"retry_count\": 0, \"created_at\": \"2026-05-20T08:37:58.013Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-20T09:26:30.567Z\", \"hypothesis\": \"SJ-weighted entity construction serves as control for VC — if SJ (soft entity overlap) works as well as VC (exact match consensus), any stronger QE signal suffices for construction; if VC specifically outperforms SJ, exact-match consensus has a unique advantage over soft similarity for entity-level selection.\", \"metrics\": {\"total_configs\": 26, \"significant_positive\": 1, \"significant_negative\": 15, \"not_significant\": 10, \"best_config\": \"scierc/72b/zs +2.0pp p=0.001\", \"worst_config\": \"fewnerd/8b_qwen3/ft mean -1.48pp all seeds p=1.0\", \"qwen3_8b_ft_supplement\": {\"configs\": 9, \"sig+\": 0, \"sig-\": 6, \"ns\": 3, \"fewnerd_mean_delta\": -1.48, \"scierc_mean_delta\": -0.4, \"conll_mean_delta\": -0.5, \"note\": \"SJ fully negative on Qwen3-8B FT — consistent with Qwen2.5-7B FT pattern\"}, \"m030_all_passed\": true}, \"notes\": \"COMPLETE (26 configs). SJ-weighted construction: 1 sig+ / 15 sig- / 10 ns. Qwen3-8B FT supplement (9 configs): 0+/6-/3ns — fully negative, consistent with 7B FT pattern. FewNERD -1.48pp mean (all seeds p=1.0). Definitively answers control question: NOT any stronger QE signal works for construction. SJ introduces noise at entity level. Only exception: SciERC 72B ZS (lowest precision, lowest degeneracy).
